Let me look at the card.md too, and check the environment.

The task: prove strong normalization for CBV STLC with locally nameless representation, using a logical relation.

Key challenges:
1. The language has no `if-then-else`, only Bool values, abs, app.
2. CBV semantics: `ST_AppAbs` requires `locally_closed (tm_abs T t)` and `value v`, and result is `open t v`.
3. `ST_App1` requires `locally_closed t2`.
4. `ST_App2` requires `value v1`.

The logical relation for strong normalization typically:
- `sn t := strongly_normalizing t`
- `R_Bool t := sn t /\ (t -->* tm_true \/ t -->* tm_false)` or just `sn t`? Actually with CBV and only Bool values, a closed well-typed term of type Bool reduces to true or false. But for strong normalization of closed terms, we can just use `sn` for Bool, and for arrow: `R (T1 -> T2) f := sn f /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (f u)` — but careful: in CBV, applying a closed value `f` of arrow type to argument `u`; if `f` is closed and value of arrow type, it is an abstraction `lambda T1 t`, and `f u --> open t u`. So the LR needs closedness or we need to handle open terms.

Since we're in locally nameless with `T_Abs` requiring all `x not in L`, the standard approach: define LR on closed terms? Actually the fundamental theorem for locally nameless: for any context Gamma, term t with `Gamma |-- t \in T`, and any substitution/valuation for free variables... Hmm, but t may have free variables, plus bvars in body of abs under binders.

Standard approach in locally nameless (e.g., from Software Foundations / Charguéraud's tutorial): Use "locally closed terms" and a relation on closed terms. But actually, the LR arrow's second component should be about applying f to an argument u. In CBV, evaluation is left-to-right: f u steps f's reducts, then u's reducts, then beta. The standard technique: define

`LR (T1->T2) f := sn f /\ forall u, LR T1 u -> LR T2 (f u)`

and prove `LR T u -> sn u` (for all T) — that's the "LR implies SN" lemma, which for arrow holds since LR includes sn.

This is a "backwards closure" argument: prove `LR T2 (app (abs body) u)` by induction on `sn u` with case analysis: either u is a value (then step to open body u, need LR T2 (open body u)), or u steps to u' with sn u' and IH gives LR T2 (app (abs body) u') and backwards closure gives LR T2 (app (abs body) u) since app (abs body) u --> app (abs body) u'. Alternatively define LR with a "value" component: many CBV logical relations use two relations: `LR T t` for arbitrary terms and `VR T v` for values. That's the cleanest for CBV. Let's think about that.

Standard CBV logical relation for STLC (with values):
- `V_Bool = {true, false}` (values of type Bool)
- `V_(T1->T2) = { λx.t | forall v ∈ V_T1, LR_T2 (t[v/x]) }`
- `LR_T = { t | exists v, t -->* v and v ∈ V_T }` — "terminating at a value".

Strong normalization: if every t in LR_T is SN. For CBV STLC, well-typed terms are in LR (and thus SN). This is the standard "terminates with a value" relation; it's much cleaner for CBV because of the value condition in beta, and the ST_App2 case.

I think the cleanest is the standard CBV "terminating at a value" relation with VR:

```
Inductive VR : ty -> tm -> Prop := (* only for values *)
  | VR_Bool_true : VR Bool tm_true
  | VR_Bool_false : VR Bool tm_false
  | VR_Arrow : forall T1 T2 body, locally_closed (tm_abs T1 body) -> (forall v, VR T1 v -> LR T2 (open body v)) -> VR (T1->T2) (tm_abs T1 body)
with LR : ty -> tm -> Prop :=
  | LR_intro : forall T t v, t -->* v -> VR T v -> LR T t.
```

Then fundamental theorem: `Gamma |-- t \in T -> forall rho, env_sem Gamma rho -> LR T (subst rho t)`, where env_sem requires `VR T (rho x)`, i.e., rho maps each variable to a *closed value* of the right type. Since rho x are values, they're locally closed (v_abs requires local closure).

Let's now design carefully. I want to minimize pain. Let me consider the "reduction to a value" LR formulation more concretely, and check we can prove everything.

Definitions:
```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => exists body, v = tm_abs T1 body /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T1 body) /\
        (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w))   (* need mutual recursion *)
  end
with LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  exists v, multi step t v /\ VR T v.
```

```
Lemma max_list_ge : forall L x, In x L -> x <= max_list L.
```

Simpler with max. Let's use `fold_right Nat.max 0 L`:
```
Lemma in_le_fold_max : forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [|a L IH]; simpl; intros x Hx.
  - destruct Hx.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (fold_right Nat.max 0 L); [apply IH; auto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L)).
  intros H. pose proof (in_le_fold_max L _ H). lia.
Qed.
```

**A. List/nat basics**: fresh atom lemma (with fv).

**B. fv function**:
```
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs T t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true | tm_false => []
  end.
```

```
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
```

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

Definition subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.
```

Let me define `lc_at_mono`:
```coq
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall t k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
Proof.
  intros t k k' Hle H. revert k' Hle.
  induction H; intros k' Hle.
  - constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; [apply IHH1 | apply IHH2]; lia.
  - constructor. apply IHH. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```

Now `lc_open_rec`:
```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec_gen : forall n t, lc_at n t ->
  forall k u, n = S k -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  intros n t H. induction H; intros k u Hn Hu; simpl.
  - subst n. destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. exact Hu.
    + constructor. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; [apply IHH1 with (k:=k) | apply IHH2 with (k:=k)]; auto.
  - (* lc_abs: H : lc_at (S n) t1, IH : forall k u, S n = S k -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t1) *)
    constructor. 
    apply IHH with (k := S k) (u := u).
    + lia. (* S n = S (S k) from n = S k *) — need n = S k; lia handles with Hn.
    + apply lc_at_mono with (k := k); [lia | exact Hu].
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.

Lemma lc_open_rec : forall k u t, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof. intros. eapply lc_open_rec_gen; eauto. Qed.
```
Hmm, in the abs case the goal after `constructor` is `lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t1)`; applying `IHH` (which is `forall k u, S n = S k -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t1)`) with k := S k gives goal `lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t1)` ✓. Side conditions: `S n = S (S k)` — from Hn : n = S k, lia ✓; and `lc_at (S k) u` from Hu : lc_at k u via mono ✓.

Great.

Now `lc_open_rec_inv`:
```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec_inv : forall k u t, lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof.
  intros k u t H. revert k u.
  induction t; intros k u H; simpl in H.
  - (* bvar n *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. constructor. lia.
    + apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. inversion H; subst. constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - inversion H; subst. constructor; auto.
  - inversion H; subst. constructor. apply IHt with (u := u). assumption.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```

`lc_subst`:
```coq
Lemma lc_subst : forall t k, lc_at k t -> forall sigma, (forall x, lc_at k (sigma x)) -> lc_at k (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros t. induction t; intros k Hk sigma Hs; simpl.
  - (* bvar *) inversion Hk; subst; constructor; assumption.
  - (* fvar *) apply Hs.
  - constructor; auto.
  - constructor; apply IHt; auto.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma multi_trans : forall t1 t2 t3, multi step t1 t2 -> multi step t2 t3 -> multi step t1 t3.
```

- multi_step x y z Hxy Hyz IH: goal multi step x t3; apply multi_step with y; [Hxy | apply IH; assumption] ✓.

Now VR/LR:

```coq
Fixpoint VR (T : ty) (v : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      exists body : tm,
        v = tm_abs T1 body /\
        lc_at 1 body /\
        (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w))
  end
with LR (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  lc_at 0 t /\ exists v, multi step t v /\ VR T v.
```

Hmm: mutual fixpoint where `VR` and `LR` both take T as the structurally decreasing argument. `LR T2 (open body w)` — T2 is a subterm of T (when T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2) ✓. `VR T1 w` subterm ✓. In LR's body, `VR T v` — same T. Coq's guard checker: mutual fixpoints, the "principal" recursive argument must decrease. Call from LR T to VR T is not decreasing but the cycle LR T -> VR T -> LR T2 decreases. However, Coq's guard condition historically requires each individual recursive call to be on a subterm of the *same* function's argument, allowing calls to the other function only if... Actually, for mutual fixpoints, Coq checks: each call to a function in the block must have the recursive argument structurally smaller than the caller's corresponding argument, EXCEPT possibly... Hmm, I recall that in mutual fixpoints, calls like `f T` from `g T` are allowed as long as the overall recursion is justified by the "decreasing" analysis? Let me recall precisely: Coq's guard condition for mutual fixpoints builds a call graph where an edge is "strict" if the argument is a strict subterm and "non-strict" if equal. It requires that every cycle in the call graph contains a strict edge. I'm fairly confident this is how it works (that's how many developments define mutual Fixpoints). Let's test.

If it fails, alternatives:
- Define a single function returning a pair/Prop conjunction: `Fixpoint LRV (T) : (tm -> Prop) * (tm -> Prop)` with projections LR := fst, VR := snd. Then recursion is structural on T and calls fst/snd of recursive results. That works but is clunky (need to destruct the recursive call).
- Or define VR by recursion on T, using LR as a parameter (Section variable) — but LR is defined in terms of VR... The mutual is the way.

Hmm, another alternative that avoids mutual recursion: note LR T t = lc t ∧ exists v, multi t v ∧ VR T v. We could inline VR into LR:

```
Fixpoint LR T t :=
  match T with
  | Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ exists v, multi step t v /\ (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false)
  | Arrow T1 T2 => lc_at 0 t /\ exists body, multi step t (tm_abs T1 body) /\ lc_at 1 body /\
                    (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w))
  end
```
Still needs VR. Define VR T v := match T with Bool => v = true \/ v = false | Arrow T1 T2 => exists body, v = abs T1 body /\ lc_at 1 body /\ (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w)) end. Mutual again.

```coq
Lemma LR_app : forall T1 T2 t1 t2,
```

Now let's think about `fresh_atom`. We need `L ++ fv t1` where L : list atom. Let me define:

```coq
Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma in_max_list : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hx; simpl.
  - destruct Hx.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eauto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_list l)).
  intros Hx. pose proof (in_max_list l _ Hx). lia.
Qed.
```

As feared, the mutual fixpoint with the same T is rejected. Need an alternative.

Options:
1. Inline VR's arrow body into LR so that the mutual recursion is only decreasing. That is, don't call VR T from LR; instead define LR directly with VR-like structure:

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => exists v, multi step t v /\ (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      exists body, multi step t (tm_abs T1 body) /\
        lc_at 1 body /\
        (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w))
  end
with VR (T : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      exists body, v = tm_abs T1 body /\ lc_at 1 body /\
        (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w))
  end.
```
Here LR T doesn't call VR T; only VR T1 and LR T2 (subterms). And VR T doesn't call LR T (only VR T1, LR T2 subterms). So all recursive calls are on strict subterms. The guard checker should accept this since both functions recurse on subterms of T.

But then LR's definition is the unfolded VR. That's fine: `LR T t = lc? ∧ ...` — I dropped lc from LR; let me include `lc_at 0 t` too.

Let me define:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  lc_at 0 t /\
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => exists v, multi step t v /\ (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      exists body, multi step t (tm_abs T1 body) /\
        lc_at 1 body /\
        (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w))
  end
with VR (T : ty) (v : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      exists body, v = tm_abs T1 body /\ lc_at 1 body /\
        (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w))
  end.
```
Wait: the recursive calls in LR: `VR T1 w` (T1 subterm of Ty_Arrow T1 T2 = T ✓), `LR T2 (...)` (T2 subterm ✓). In VR: `VR T1 w`, `LR T2 (...)` ✓. So all calls decrease. But now VR is defined *after* LR in the mutual block; that's fine.

Hmm, but there's a subtlety: the two definitions must both be structurally decreasing on their own principal argument, and Coq checks each function separately but allows mutual. Let me test.

But this duplicates the Boolean case and the arrow structure. It's fine. Lemma VR_LR: need LR T v from VR T v: LR requires lc_at 0 v and the unfolded match. We can prove by induction on T:
- Bool: VR gives v = true ∨ v = false; LR: lc (true/false) ✓, and exists v' multi v v' ∧ (v' = true ∨ v' = false): take v' = v, multi_refl ✓.
- Arrow: VR gives body with v = abs T1 body, lc_at 1 body, Hsem. LR: lc (abs) ✓ from lc body; exists body' multi v (abs T1 body') ... take body' = body, multi_refl ✓, lc body ✓, Hsem ✓.

Proving this by induction on T requires unfolding LR/VR and the induction hypotheses at T1, T2 (for the Hsem part? no, Hsem is given). Actually for VR→LR at arrow, we only need the components given in VR — no IH needed except for lc? lc_at 0 (abs T1 body) ← lc_abs with lc_at 1 body ✓. So VR→LR doesn't need induction at all, just unfolding. Similarly LR→VR? We need: LR T t → exists v, multi t v ∧ VR T v — that's part of LR's definition; for arrow, LR gives multi t (abs T1 body) and the VR arrow requires exactly `v = abs T1 body ∧ lc_at 1 body ∧ Hsem` — which matches. So `LR_to_VR : LR T t -> exists v, multi step t v ∧ VR T v` holds by unfolding: for Bool, LR gives exists v, multi t v ∧ (v = true ∨ v = false) and VR Bool v = same ✓. For Arrow, LR gives exists body, multi t (abs T1 body) ∧ lc ∧ Hsem, and VR arrow (abs T1 body) = exists body', abs T1 body = abs T1 body' ∧ ... take body' = body ✓. Need to handle the `match T with` in LR after the conjunction. Since LR is defined with `lc_at 0 t /\ match T with ...`, destructing T first will simplify.

Let me structure the definitions to make destructing T easy:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => lc_at 0 t /\ exists v, multi step t v /\ (v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => lc_at 0 t /\ exists body, multi step t (tm_abs T1 body) /\ lc_at 1 body /\

  end
with VR (T : ty) (v : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => exists body, v = tm_abs T1 body /\ lc_at 1 body /\
        (forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w))
  end.
```
Then `destruct T; simpl in H` works nicely.

```coq
(* ============================================================ *)
(* Auxiliary definitions                                        *)
(* ============================================================ *)

(** Free variables of a term (as a list). *)
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  end.

(** Maximum of a list of atoms, for freshness. *)
Fixpoint max_atom (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_atom xs)
  end.

Lemma in_max_atom : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_atom l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hx; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hx.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eauto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_atom l)).
  intros Hx. pose proof (in_max_atom l _ Hx). lia.
Qed.
```
Need `Lia`.

```coq
(** Term substitution for free variables. *)
Fixpoint subst_tm (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_tm sigma t1) (subst_tm sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_tm sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  end.

Definition update_env (rho : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (w : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then w else rho y.

Lemma update_env_same : forall rho x w, update_env rho x w x = w.
Proof. intros. unfold update_env. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma update_env_other : forall rho x w y, x <> y -> update_env rho x w y = rho y.
Proof.
  intros rho x w y Hne. unfold update_env.
  destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. congruence.
  - reflexivity.
Qed.
```

Hmm, `Nat.eqb x y = true -> x = y` is `Nat.eqb_eq`. ✓

```coq
Lemma subst_tm_fvar : forall sigma x, subst_tm sigma (tm_fvar x) = sigma x.
Proof. reflexivity. Qed.
Lemma subst_tm_open_rec : forall sigma k u t,
  subst_tm sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst_tm sigma u) (subst_tm sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma k u t. revert k u.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall t k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.

  - constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; [apply IHH1 | apply IHH2]; lia.

  - constructor.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec_gen : forall n t, lc_at n t ->
  forall k u, n = S k -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
Proof.
  intros n t H. induction H; intros k u Hn Hu; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. exact Hu.
    + constructor. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; [apply IHH1 with (k := k) | apply IHH2 with (k := k)]; auto.
  - constructor. apply IHH with (k := S k).
    + lia.
    + apply lc_at_mono with (k := k); [lia | exact Hu].
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```
Hmm, in the lc_bvar case: H : i < n, Hn : n = S k, E : (k =? i) = false. Goal: lc_at k (if k =? i then u else tm_bvar i). With E false: lc_at k (tm_bvar i) → constructor → i < k. From H : i < n = S k and E: k ≠ i → i ≠ k... wait, `Nat.eqb_neq : forall x y, (x =? y) = false -> x <> y`, so k ≠ i. With i < S k and k ≠ i, we get i < k ✓ lia.

Applying with k0 := S k gives goal `lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t1)` ✓ and side conditions `S n = S (S k)` (from Hn : n = S k → lia ✓) and `lc_at (S k) u` (from Hu via mono ✓).

Applying with k := S k leaves goals: `S n = S (S k)`, `lc_at (S k) u`, and the conclusion `lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t1)` which should match the goal ✓.
```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec : forall k u t, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
```

```coq
Lemma lc_open_rec_inv : forall k u t, lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t.
Proof.
  intros k u t H. revert k u.
  induction t; intros k u H; simpl in H.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. constructor. lia.
    + constructor. apply Nat.eqb_neq in E. 
      (* H : lc_at k (tm_bvar n) with E false *)
      inversion H; subst. lia.
  - constructor.
  - inversion H; subst. constructor; auto.
  - inversion H; subst. constructor. apply IHt with (u := u). assumption.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```

Wait, `fresh_atom` returns `exists x, ~ In x l`, so destruct gives x and Hx : ~ In x (L ++ fv t1). Then HxL : ~ In x L by intro + apply Hx + in_or_app left; Hxfv similarly.

```
               + apply VR_lc with (T := T1). exact Hw.

           (* simpl: subst_tm rho' (tm_fvar x) = rho' x = w *)

           assert (Hsubst : subst_tm rho' t1 = subst_tm rho t1).
```

Careful: `open` is defined as `open_rec 0 u t` where open t u. So `open (subst_tm rho t1) w = open_rec 0 w (subst_tm rho t1)` ✓ definitionally.

Note: `rewrite subst_tm_open_rec in Hlr` — the lemma is `subst_tm sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst_tm sigma u) (subst_tm sigma t)`; in Hlr, the term is `subst_tm rho' (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t1)` — matches with k=0, u = tm_fvar x, t = t1 ✓.

Now `LR_app` proof in detail:

```coq
Lemma LR_to_VR : forall T t, LR T t -> exists v, multi step t v /\ VR T v.
Proof.
  intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H.
  - destruct H as [Hlc [v [Hm Hv]]]. exists v. split; assumption.
  - destruct H as [Hlc [body [Hm [Hlcbody Hsem]]]]. exists (tm_abs T1 body). simpl. 
    split; [exact Hm | ]. split; [reflexivity | split; assumption].
Qed.
```
Hmm, for the arrow case, `exists (tm_abs T1 body)` then need `VR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 body)`; `simpl` unfolds VR (with head constructor known) to `exists body', tm_abs T1 body = tm_abs T1 body' /\ ...`. Then `exists body` and split reflexivity... Let me write:
```
  - destruct H as [Hlc [body [Hm [Hlcbody Hsem]]]].
    exists (tm_abs T1 body).
    split; [exact Hm |].
    simpl. exists body. split; [reflexivity | split; assumption].
```
Careful: after `simpl`, the goal is `VR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 body)`; `simpl` should reduce it because the first argument starts with a constructor ✓.

```
      * eapply multi_trans.
        -- eapply multi_app_l. exact Hm1. apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact H2.
        -- eapply multi_app_r. exact Hval_body. exact Hm2.
```

Wait, the goal after `split` in the arrow case: `LR T2 (tm_app t1 t2)` — but T2 is a variable! So `split` doesn't apply: LR T2 (app t1 t2) is a Fixpoint application on the variable T2, not a conjunction. We must destruct T2 first.

Right: `destruct T2` before splitting. Let me restructure:

```
  destruct T2 as [| T2a T2b].
  - (* T2 = Ty_Bool *)
    simpl. simpl in Hsem. split...
```
Hmm, Hsem : forall w, VR T1 w -> LR T2 (open body w) — with T2 substituted. And Hlr : LR T2 (open body v2). Let me destruct T2 early, right at the start:

```coq
Lemma LR_app : forall T1 T2 t1 t2,
  LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1 -> LR T1 t2 -> LR T2 (tm_app t1 t2).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 t1 t2 H1 H2.
  simpl in H1.
  destruct H1 as [Hlc1 [body [Hm1 [Hlcbody Hsem]]]].
  destruct (LR_to_VR T1 t2 H2) as [v2 [Hm2 HV2]].
  assert (Hval_body : value (tm_abs T1 body)) by (constructor; exact Hlcbody).
  assert (Hval2 : value v2) by (apply VR_value; exact HV2).
  assert (Hlr : LR T2 (open body v2)) by (apply Hsem; exact HV2).
  destruct (LR_to_VR T2 (open body v2) Hlr) as [v3 [Hm3 HV3]].
  destruct T2 as [| T2a T2b].
  - (* T2 = Bool *)
    simpl. simpl in HV3.
    split.
    + simpl. split; [exact Hlc1 | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact H2].
    + exists v3. split.
      * eapply multi_trans; [ | exact Hm3].
        eapply multi_trans.
        -- eapply multi_app_l; [exact Hm1 | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact H2].
        -- eapply multi_app_r; [exact Hval_body | exact Hm2].
      * exact HV3.
  - (* T2 = Arrow *)
    simpl. split.
    + simpl. split; [exact Hlc1 | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact H2].
    + (* exists body', multi (app t1 t2) (abs T2a body') /\ ... *)
      ...
```
This is getting repetitive. The two branches produce the same structure: LR T2 (app t1 t2) = lc (app t1 t2) ∧ exists v, multi (app t1 t2) v ∧ VR T2 v (in both branches, the "exists v" form!). Indeed for both Bool and Arrow, LR T2 (app t1 t2) unfolds to `lc_at 0 (app t1 t2) ∧ exists v, multi step (app t1 t2) v ∧ <VR-ish>`. For Bool: `exists v, multi ... v ∧ (v = true ∨ v = false)` = `exists v, multi ... v ∧ VR Bool v` ✓ definitionally. For Arrow: `exists body, multi ... (abs T2a body) ∧ lc body ∧ Hsem` vs `exists v, multi ... v ∧ VR arrow v` = `exists v, multi ... v ∧ (exists body, v = abs T2a body ∧ lc body ∧ Hsem)`. These are logically equivalent but not definitionally the same shape... but wait, for the *proof* I don't need to match the shape: I need to prove `LR T2 (app t1 t2)` after destructing T2, and in each case I can use the explicit form.

Alternative cleaner: state and prove a lemma `LR_intro_pack`:
```
Lemma LR_pack : forall T t v, lc_at 0 t -> multi step t v -> VR T v -> LR T t.
```
Is this provable uniformly? For T = Bool: LR = lc ∧ exists v, multi t v ∧ (v = true ∨ v = false) — given VR Bool v = (v = true ∨ v = false) ✓. For T = Arrow: VR (T1->T2) v = exists body, v = abs T1 body ∧ lc ∧ Hsem; LR arrow t = lc t ∧ exists body, multi t (abs T1 body) ∧ lc ∧ Hsem. Given multi t v and v = abs T1 body → multi t (abs T1 body) ✓. So yes! Uniform proof by destruct T; simpl; destruct the VR hypothesis appropriately; auto. 

```coq
Lemma LR_pack : forall T t v,
  lc_at 0 t -> multi step t v -> VR T v -> LR T t.
Proof.
  intros T t v Hlc Hm HV. destruct T; simpl.
  - simpl in HV. split; [exact Hlc | exists v; split; assumption].
  - simpl in HV. destruct HV as [body [-> [Hlcbody Hsem]]].
    split; [exact Hlc | exists body; split; [exact Hm | split; assumption]].
Qed.
```
Hmm, careful: `destruct HV as [body [-> ...]]` with `->` substitutes v := tm_abs T1 body in the goal ✓. But Hm : multi step t v becomes multi step t (tm_abs T1 body) ✓.

Wait, in the Bool case, `VR Ty_Bool v` after simpl is `v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false` ✓; LR Bool t after simpl = `lc_at 0 t ∧ exists v0, multi step t v0 ∧ (v0 = tm_true ∨ v0 = tm_false)` ✓; split; [Hlc | exists v; split; assumption] ✓.

Great, `LR_pack` makes LR_app clean:

```coq
Lemma LR_app : forall T1 T2 t1 t2,
  LR (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1 -> LR T1 t2 -> LR T2 (tm_app t1 t2).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 t1 t2 H1 H2.
  simpl in H1.
  destruct H1 as [Hlc1 [body [Hm1 [Hlcbody Hsem]]]].
  destruct (LR_to_VR T1 t2 H2) as [v2 [Hm2 HV2]].
  assert (Hval_body : value (tm_abs T1 body)) by (constructor; exact Hlcbody).
  assert (Hval2 : value v2) by (apply VR_value; exact HV2).
  assert (Hlr : LR T2 (open body v2)) by (apply Hsem; exact HV2).
  destruct (LR_to_VR T2 (open body v2) Hlr) as [v3 [Hm3 HV3]].
  apply LR_pack with (v := v3).
  - simpl. split; [exact Hlc1 | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact H2].
  - eapply multi_trans; [ | exact Hm3].
    eapply multi_trans.
    + eapply multi_app_l; [exact Hm1 | apply LR_lc with (T := T1); exact H2].
    + eapply multi_app_r; [exact Hval_body | exact Hm2].
  - exact HV3.
Qed.
```
✓ Nice.

Note: LR_pack is stated with `forall T t v, lc t -> multi t v -> VR T v -> LR T t`.

Now VR_value, VR_lc, VR_LR, LR_lc, LR_to_VR, LR_pack, LR_sn.

```coq
Lemma VR_value : forall T v, VR T v -> value v.
Proof.
  intros T v H. destruct T; simpl in H.
  - destruct H as [-> | ->]; constructor.
  - destruct H as [body [-> [Hlc _]]]. constructor. exact Hlc.
Qed.

Lemma VR_lc : forall T v, VR T v -> lc_at 0 v.
Proof.
  intros T v H. destruct T; simpl in H.
  - destruct H as [-> | ->]; constructor.
  - destruct H as [body [-> [Hlc _]]]. constructor. exact Hlc.
Qed.

Lemma VR_LR : forall T v, VR T v -> LR T v.
Proof.
  intros T v H. destruct T; simpl in H |- *.
  - destruct H as [-> | ->]; split.
    + constructor.
    + exists tm_true; split; [apply multi_refl | left; reflexivity].
    ... 
```
Hmm, careful: destructing H with `[-> | ->]` substitutes v. Let me do it more uniformly with LR_pack:
`VR_LR T v H = LR_pack T v v (VR_lc T v H) (multi_refl v) H`. Since LR_pack requires lc_at 0 v, multi v v, VR T v ✓. 

```coq
Lemma VR_LR : forall T v, VR T v -> LR T v.
Proof.
  intros T v H. apply LR_pack with (v := v).
  - apply VR_lc. exact H.
  - apply multi_refl.
  - exact H.
Qed.
```
✓ 

```coq
Lemma LR_lc : forall T t, LR T t -> lc_at 0 t.
Proof.
  intros T t H. destruct T; simpl in H; destruct H; assumption.
Qed.
```
Hmm, for T = arrow, after simpl H is `lc /\ exists ...`; destruct gives lc ✓. For Bool same ✓. But `destruct H; assumption` — after destruct, the first component is exactly `lc_at 0 t`; `assumption` ✓. Actually simpler: `apply (proj1 H)` after simpl. Let me keep `destruct T; simpl in H; apply (proj1 H)`.

```coq
Lemma LR_sn : forall T t, LR T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T t H.
  destruct (LR_to_VR T t H) as [v [Hm HV]].
  eapply multi_sn; [exact Hm |].
  intros w Hst. exact (value_no_step v w (VR_value T v HV) Hst).
Qed.
```

`multi_sn`:
```coq
Lemma multi_sn : forall t v, multi step t v -> (forall w, ~ step v w) -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t v H. induction H; intros Hnf.
  - apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst. exfalso. apply (Hnf t' Hst).
  - apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst.
    assert (t' = y) as -> by (eapply step_det; eauto).
    apply IHmulti. exact Hnf.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `assert (t' = y) as ->` — the syntax `assert (t' = y) as ->` isn't valid; use `assert (Heq : t' = y) by (eapply step_det; eauto). subst t'. apply IHmulti. exact Hnf.` Wait, but IHmulti : (forall w, ~ step v w) -> sn y — need to check the induction: H : multi step t v with constructors; `induction H` gives, in the multi_step case: y is the intermediate, and Hmulti : multi step y v, IHmulti : (forall w, ~ step v w) -> sn y ✓.

In the multi_refl case: t = v; goal sn v given Hnf : forall w, ~ step v w. SN_intro: intros t' Hst; exfalso; apply Hnf; exact Hst ✓.

In the multi_step case: goal sn t (where t = x). SN_intro: intros t' Hst; Hstep : step t y; step_det Hstep Hst gives t' = y; subst; apply IHmulti; exact Hnf ✓.

Careful: `step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2`. So `eapply step_det; eauto` with Hst : step t t' and Hstep : step t y gives t' = y? step_det Hstep Hst : y = t', or step_det Hst Hstep : t' = y. Let me write explicit: `assert (Heq : t' = y) by (eapply step_det; [exact Hst | exact Hstep])` hmm, step_det t t' y Hst Hstep : t' = y ✓. So `apply (step_det t t' y Hst Hstep)`? The lemma is `step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2`; so `step_det t t' y Hst Hstep : t' = y` ✓.

Let me write: `assert (Heq : t' = y) by (eapply step_det; eauto). subst t'. apply IHmulti. exact Hnf.` — `eapply step_det` with goal `t' = y` unifies t1 := t', t2 := y? The conclusion is `?t1 = ?t2`, goal `t' = y`, so t1 := t', t2 := y, then subgoals `step ?t t'` and `step ?t y`, solved by eauto with Hst and Hstep ✓.

Now `has_type_lc`:
```coq
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> lc_at 0 t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H as [Gamma x T Hvar
                                     | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
                                     | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                                     | Gamma
                                     | Gamma].
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
    destruct (fresh_atom L) as [x Hx].
    apply lc_open_rec_inv with (u := tm_fvar x) (k := 0).
    apply IHbody. exact Hx.
  - constructor; assumption.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
Qed.
```
Check the T_Abs case: goal `lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1)`; constructor gives `lc_at 1 t1`; `lc_open_rec_inv` with k=0, u=fvar x: `lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) -> lc_at 1 t1` ✓; IHbody x Hx : lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x)) = lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) ✓.

Wait: is `lc_open_rec_inv` stated as `lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t`, so with k=0 gives `lc_at 1 t` from `lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 u t)` ✓.

Also: does `induction H` here work with the motive `fun Gamma t T => lc_at 0 t`? I need to give the intro pattern; the default naming is fine but I need to refer to IHbody. Let me use the `as` pattern.

Hmm, one potential snag: The T_Abs branch in the induction principle has both the original premise and the IH (as seen in test3). Actually in test3 the printed principle showed both `_ : forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...` and `_ : forall x, ~ In x L -> True`. Interesting — so the branch has 7 arguments. Theorem normalization proof:
```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (Henv : env_sem empty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
  { split.
    - intros x. constructor.
    - intros x T' Hc. unfold empty in Hc. discriminate. }
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) Henv) as Hlr.
  rewrite subst_tm_id in Hlr.
  apply LR_sn in Hlr. exact Hlr.
Qed.
```

Wait: `fundamental empty t T Hty rho Henv` — check argument order: `forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall rho, env_sem Gamma rho -> LR T (subst_tm rho t)` ✓.

`rewrite subst_tm_id in Hlr` — the term is `subst_tm (fun x => tm_fvar x) t`; subst_tm_id : forall t, subst_tm (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t ✓.

Alternatively — here's a cleaner idea: prove determinism by a *nested* induction that mirrors the syntax, using the following robust structure:

```coq
Lemma step_det : forall t u1 u2, step t u1 -> step t u2 -> u1 = u2.
Proof.
  intros t. induction t; intros u1 u2 H1 H2.
  - (* bvar *) inversion H1.
  - (* fvar *) inversion H1.
  - (* app *)
    inversion H1 as [Ta ba va Hlca Hva | a a' b Hsa Hlcb | b1 b2 b2' Hvb Hsb]; subst.
    + inversion H2 as [Ta' ba' va' Hlca' Hva' | a'' a''' b'' Hsa' Hlcb' | b1' b2'' b2''' Hvb' Hsb']; subst.
      * reflexivity.
      * exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [constructor; exact Hlca | exact Hsa'].
      * exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hva | exact Hsb'].
    + inversion H2 as [Ta' ba' va' Hlca' Hva' | a'' a''' b'' Hsa' Hlcb' | b1' b2'' b2''' Hvb' Hsb']; subst.
      * exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [constructor; exact Hlca' | exact Hsa].
      * f_equal. eapply IHt1; eauto.
      * exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hvb' | exact Hsa].
    + inversion H2 as [Ta' ba' va' Hlca' Hva' | a'' a''' b'' Hsa' Hlcb' | b1' b2'' b2''' Hvb' Hsb']; subst.
      * exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hvb | exact Hsb].
      * exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hvb | exact Hsa'].
      * f_equal. eapply IHt2; eauto.
  - (* abs *) inversion H1.
  - (* true *) inversion H1.
  - (* false *) inversion H1.
Qed.
```

Now, let me spend a bit of effort on robustness/quality review of the proof, in case there's a subtle logical flaw that Coq accepted due to some definitional subtlety. Let me re-derive the key soundness points:

1. **LR definition**: 
```
LR T t := 
  Bool: lc_at 0 t ∧ ∃v. t -->* v ∧ (v = true ∨ v = false)
  Arrow T1 T2: lc_at 0 t ∧ ∃body. t -->* abs T1 body ∧ lc_at 1 body ∧ ∀w. VR T1 w → LR T2 (open body w)
VR T v :=
  Bool: v = true ∨ v = false
  Arrow T1 T2: ∃body. v = abs T1 body ∧ lc_at 1 body ∧ ∀w. VR T1 w → LR T2 (open body w)
```
✓ This is a standard CBV logical relation ("terminates at a value") with the extra local-closure component.

2. **LR → SN**: LR T t gives t -->* v with VR T v; VR → value → irreducible; determinism gives SN. Is this sound? The argument: if t -->* v and v is irreducible, and step is deterministic, then every reduct of t reduces to v, and the multistep derivation from t to v has a fixed length; any reduction sequence from t is a prefix of the deterministic path to v (since at each step there's exactly one reduct). Formal proof by induction on the multistep derivation:
- refl: t = v, v irreducible → SN.
- step: t --> y, y -->* v. For any t' with t --> t': determinism gives t' = y; by IH (y -->* v, shorter derivation) sn y; hence sn t.
✓ Correct. Note the induction is on the multistep derivation, and in the step case, IH applies to the tail (y -->* v). ✓

3. **LR_app**: given LR arrow t1 and LR T1 t2, show LR T2 (app t1 t2). The chain: t1 -->* abs body; t2 -->* v2 (a value in VR T1); then:
- app t1 t2 -->* app (abs body) t2 using ST_App1 (needs lc t2 ✓ from LR T1 t2).
- app (abs body) t2 -->* app (abs body) v2 using ST_App2 (needs value (abs body) ✓ from lc body).
- app (abs body) v2 --> open body v2 using ST_AppAbs (needs lc (abs body) ✓ and value v2 ✓).
- open body v2 → v3 with VR T2 v3 from Hsem v2 HV2.
Combine ✓. And lc (app t1 t2) ✓. Correct.

Wait — one subtle thing in `multi_app_l`: I use it with Hm1 : multi step t1 (tm_abs T1 body) and lc t2. ✓.

4. **Fundamental theorem**: by induction on typing.
- T_Var: subst rho (fvar x) = rho x; env gives LR ✓.
- T_Abs: goal LR (T1->T2) (abs T1 (subst rho t1)).
  - lc: lc_at 0 (abs T1 body) from lc_at 1 body ✓ (via lc_subst with k=1 needing lc_at 1 t1 from typing→lc and lc of rho images from env).
  - exists body' = body, multi_refl, lc, and ∀w VR T1 w, LR T2 (open body w): proved using IH at a fresh x with the extended environment; uses subst_tm_open_rec (with lc images), update_env_same, subst_tm_update_notin (freshness w.r.t. fv t1). ✓
- T_App: LR_app ✓.
- T_True/T_False ✓.

5. **Final theorem**: env_sem empty identity; subst identity; LR_sn ✓.

The freshness argument: `fresh_atom (L ++ fv t1)` gives x ∉ L and x ∉ fv t1 ✓. The T_Abs premise requires `~ In x L` ✓.

`has_type_lc`: by induction on typing; T_Abs needs a fresh x ∉ L; gives lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x)); inversion to lc_at 1 t1 ✓.

All sound.
